## Phases 50-51 prep (2026-11-20)

| Ticket | Question or text | Choice made | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EZD-1501 | Ticket row text (status Unreviewed/Approved, approver, type, glossary table, translation_usage table) | Built reduced. Only the `translation` table: id, tenant_id, phrase_en, text_es (null until drafted), drafted_at, created_at. No status, approver or type (nothing reviews). No glossary table (its only editor was the S16 Glossary dialog, now retired). No translation_usage table (it fed only the S16 card's "Used on" links and propagation, both retired) | 10-permissions-and-settings.md#Q-0234; 03c-tables.md translation row; 03e glossary, translation_usage row |
| EZD-1501 | "The same English phrase on two jobs uses one row": what counts as the same | One row per (tenant_id, normalised phrase_en), with a unique index. Normalised means trimmed, with every run of whitespace turned into one space. Case and punctuation are kept ("GFCI" ≠ "gfci"). Never shared across companies | AC-EZD-1501-1; CLAUDE.md rule 4 |
| EZD-1501 | Who reads and writes the rows | Rows hold phrases from every project of a company, so a Listed-scope crew member must not read them directly. RLS on, with no member select, insert, update or delete policy. The drafting job writes through server-side definer code (the review_item pattern). Phone reads get Spanish through the task's own text_es, or through a server lookup limited to phrases in the issue the caller can open. Rule 4 test: company B reads none of A's rows and cannot insert or change them. Security review | CLAUDE.md rule 4; migration 20261118231270_create_review_item.sql header; architecture "Server-only data access" |
| EZD-1501 | Test numbers | None. Row counts come from the AC: two jobs with the same phrase give 1 row; the same phrase in two companies gives 2 rows | AC-EZD-1501-1 |

| EZD-1611a | Fixture and test numbers (AC-EZD-1611a-1) | A fixture kitchen and bath committed under the packages/geometry test fixtures. The expected-quantity JSON is written by hand with the arithmetic in a comment on each line, and checked by calc-auditor before the test is written (rule 2). Worked lines to include: **Floor:** 10' × 12' = 120 SF, less two 24" × 36" base boxes (12 SF) = 108 SF; tile waste 10 + straight 0 → 118.8 SF; 118.8 / 10 = 11.88 → 12 boxes. **Drywall:** a New 10' × 8' wall drywalled on both faces = 160 SF; drywall waste 10 → 176 SF / 32 = 5.5 → 6 sheets. **Paint:** a 44' perimeter × 8' = 352 SF, less a 3' × 7' door (21 SF, over 20) = 331 SF; a 3' × 4' window (12 SF) is not deducted; paint waste 5 → 331 × 2 × 1.05 / 350 = 1.99 → 2 gal. **Base:** 44 − 3 = 41 LF × 1.10 = 45.1 LF. **Casing:** a 36" × 80" interior door, 2 × (2 × 80 + 36)" = 392" = 32.7 LF. Tests set every waste value they use | 09i; CLAUDE.md rule 2 |
| EZD-1611b | Countertops | SF per piece and exposed edge LF from EZD-418's top schedule functions (the S04b numbers); waste_countertop applies to SF. No slab count: no slab size exists in the spec or Defaults. The board's "Countertop length" and "Backsplash" lines are sample data and are not built | 09i Countertops; assumptions EZD-411 exposed edges; board |
| EZD-1611b | Cabinets, fixtures, devices | Cabinet boxes and fillers are counted by code (EZD-409's grouping, case and spaces ignored), New only. Fixtures are counted by kind and devices by device type, ceiling devices (EZD-507) included. A kind with no takeoff label falls under the unknown-type row (no line) | 09i Cabinets, fixtures, devices; assumptions EZD-409 |
| EZD-1611b | Wire per circuit | The path runs from the panel through each device in the circuit's stored order. Each leg is the shortest path along wall lines (walls joined at shared ends), with each point taken to its nearest wall line; with no joined path, the straight distance is used. Rise: once from the panel's height to the ceiling, then 2 × (ceiling height − device height) per device, with the ceiling height from the device's room (EZD-501). Multiplied by (1 + slack) and rounded up to whole feet. New Defaults row "Wire slack" (percent, default 15, electrical group) | 09i Wire; assumptions EZD-604 circuits |
| EZD-1611b | Test numbers (AC-EZD-1611b-1) | Worked by hand, as for EZD-1611a. Wire example: panel at 60" in a room with a 96" ceiling, and two receptacles at 16", at 10' and 18' along the same wall. Legs 10' + 8' = 18'. Rise (96 − 60)" + 2 × 80" + 2 × 80" = 356" = 29.67'. (18 + 29.67) × 1.15 = 54.8 → 55 LF | 09i Wire |
| EZD-1611c | ACT lines | From EZD-511's laid-out grid. ACT tile = grid cells (full and cut) × (1 + ACT waste), rounded up. Mains = across-dimension / main spacing, rounded up; pieces = ceil(total main length / 12 ft). Cross tees are counted from the grid's tee segments between mains, cut ones too; a 2 x 2 grid adds one 2 ft tee per 4 ft tee. Wall angle = ceil(perimeter / 12 ft). Hanger wire = ceil(main length / hanger spacing) per main. New Defaults rows (ceilings group): "ACT waste" (percent, default 5), "ACT main spacing" (length, default 48"), "Hanger wire spacing" (length, default 48") | 09i ACT rows |
| EZD-1611c | Test numbers (AC-EZD-1611c-1) | A 12' × 10' room, 2 x 4 grid, mains along the 12' side. Mains = ceil(10 / 4) = 3; main pieces = ceil(36 / 12) = 3; wall angle = ceil(44 / 12) = 4; hanger wire = 3 × ceil(12 / 4) = 9. Tile and tee counts are counted by hand from the EZD-511 layout of that room | 09i ACT rows |
| EZD-1611c | Option costs | The option cost lines users type (EZD-1009/1010; 13b R22), listed per option with their total. Takeoff computes no option cost of its own. They are read through the existing option routes (See company costs) and are not stored in the takeoff row | 13b-round-26.md R22; S19 Data shown "cost per option ... internal only" |
| EZD-1611c | The takeoff table | `takeoff` (id, tenant_id, project_id, model_version_id unique, lines jsonb, computed_at, schema_version). It then emits `takeoff.updated` with project, version and line count only. This covers Version N sends and Rev N issues (EZD-717 sets is_sent) without depending on EZD-717. | EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Triggers | As row 634 says. new-dwellings-only (NE2-107, NE4-126): never run, listed in the closing line. change-of-use (NE2-008's note, NE2-134): only when the project's change-of-use field is yes. whole-dwelling (NE2-001, 004, 009, NE4-121 to 123): NE4-121 to 123 test every smoke alarm in the model when R3-117 fires (EZD-617 row 1750), and New alarms otherwise. NE2-004 and NE2-117 (buildings other than one- and two-family) never run: commercial work is v2 or out of scope. NE2-090 and NE2-156 appear only when EZD-620's townhouse / two-family field says so | assumptions 634, 1750; non-goals commercial; 02-product-overview.md#Q-0767 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Definitions NE2-006, 007, 008, 010 (Automatic, no failing case) and the room each NEC rule means | They are the shared classifier the other NE rules call. Bathroom: a room that holds a lavatory or sink plus a toilet, tub, prefab shower or tile shower, whatever its type. Kitchen: a room typed Kitchen, or one that holds a sink plus a range or cooktop. Habitable: Kitchen, Living and Bedroom types; Other reads "Room type not set". Laundry area: a room typed Laundry, or one that holds a washer or dryer. The only result is an information note, "{room} is treated as a {class} for NEC rules", shown where the fixtures and the typed room disagree. AC added: for each of the four, a pair. A room typed Other holding a lavatory and a toilet gives the bathroom note, and NE2-045 runs on its receptacles. A room holding only a toilet gives no note | catalog NE2-006/007/008/010; 03-data-model.md#Q-0762; assumptions 1735 precedent |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Rooms the NEC names that Q-0762 has no type for | Dining is a Living type (Q-0762), so the small-appliance rules (NE2-063, 097–099) test Kitchen rooms. A receptacle in a Living room may be on a small-appliance circuit without a warning. A pantry or breakfast area counts as part of the Kitchen when it is drawn inside the Kitchen outline. Closet or storage counts as a clothes closet for NE3-010 and NE3-175 to 178, the stricter reading (a warning that can be accepted with a note). Foyer, sunroom, crawl space, basement, boathouse, accessory or detached building, and porch, deck or balcony have no type: see the not-drawn row | 03-data-model.md#Q-0762 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Outdoor, damp and wet locations | A device, or a condenser (EZD-731), whose point lies outside every room outline on its floor is outdoors. Room outlines are drawn to the inside finished face (Q-0559), so a receptacle on the outside face of a wall is outdoors. Outdoors counts as wet. A device in a room whose damp-location value (NE2-056's needs field) is yes is damp. Anything else is dry. One location value per device is shared by NE2-047, 056, 061; NE3-059, 066, 118, 141, 151, 152, 170 | 09-calculations-and-rules.md#Q-0559; catalog NE2-056, NE3-066, NE3-118 needs |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Which circuit an appliance or HVAC unit is on (circuit exists only on devices; utility points serve fixtures only) | An appliance is on the circuit its electric utility point (EZD-234) is assigned to. EZD-630 adds an optional `circuit` and `protection` to electric utility points, as EZD-601 did for devices, unless EZD-604 already did. Being optional, it needs no version step. EZD-630 also lets an electric utility point serve an air handler or condenser (EZD-731) as well as a fixture; if EZD-655 merged first, that widening is already in. A rule that needs an appliance's circuit gives cannot-check "{appliance} has no electric point" when there is none | electrical.ts; existing-details.ts; utility-point.ts; assumptions EZD-601, EZD-610 (served appliance's electric point), 1877 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Nameplate values for the Needs data rules (NE2-079, 085, 086, 133; NE4-014, 015, 018, 021, 022, 045, 070) | First the served electric utility point's volts and amps (EZD-234; EZD-610's own source), then the catalog item's rating (install_specs, EZD-614/1602). The EZD-614/640 common-load defaults are never used, because typical values are not a nameplate for a pass or fail check. With neither: cannot-check naming "Nameplate amps" | 09-calculations-and-rules.md#Q-0704; assumptions EZD-610, EZD-607 rating order, EZD-614 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Wire size and material | Wire size (AWG) on the circuit is EZD-604's ("breaker, wire … editable"). Material is EZD-610's (copper by default, or aluminum). Aluminum is tested only where the check text gives it (NE3-002: 12 AWG 15 A, 10 AWG 25 A). Elsewhere aluminum gives cannot-check naming Table 310.16 | phase-06 EZD-604 row; assumptions EZD-610 wire material |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | GFCI and AFCI protection | GFCI protected: the device's protection is gfci or dual, or its circuit's is (a GFCI breaker). The device field means protected at or upstream of the device; feed-through order is not modeled. AFCI protected: the circuit's protection is afci or dual, or any device on that circuit is afci or dual (an outlet branch-circuit AFCI at the first outlet; which outlet is first is not modeled). NE2-067's needs value is the circuit's protection, which reads "none" when empty (EZD-601), so it has no missing-value case (AC correction below) | electrical.ts; assumptions EZD-601 device circuit row; catalog NE2-067, NE2-069 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Distances "within 6 ft" (NE2-043 path rule; NE2-052, 054, 091, 109; NE4-099) | The shortest horizontal path from the device's point to the nearest point of the fixture outline. A sink bowl's edge is taken as the fixture or cutout outline, which is stricter. The path may cross no wall except through a door opening, and at 9th ed. also a window opening. Height is not counted. 72 in. is within 6 ft and 72 1/16 in. is not. This differs from the spa rule (EZD-660, no doorways) | catalog NE2-043 check and edition_difference; assumptions EZD-660 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Tub and shower zone (NE3-153, NE3-171, NE3-172, NE4-030, NE4-062, NE3-194) | 36 in. horizontally from the outline of a tub, prefab shower or tile shower (EZD-226), including the space directly over it. Rim and threshold heights are not modeled, so the 8 ft top of the zone is taken at the room's ceiling (EZD-501), which is stricter. A ceiling fan reaches as far as its center plus half its Blade diameter (value row below). Until that value is typed, the fan parts of NE3-171, NE4-030 and NE4-123 give cannot-check | catalog NE3-153, NE3-171, NE4-030 check text; 03f |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | One result for one defect (extends row 1740, which needs the same section) | When rules from different sections fail the same object, for the same missing property, in one run, one result is listed under the first id in catalog file order. It names the other ids and their sections. The groups are: GFCI (NE2-003, 044–056, 059–061; NE3-138; NE4-011, 094, 099); AFCI (NE2-067–069; NE3-139); wire against breaker (NE2-016, 071, 073, 089; NE3-002, 058, 099); receptacle rating (NE2-078, 080; NE3-132); tamper-resistant (NE3-140, 156); weather-resistant (NE3-141, 151); voltage drop (NE2-072, NE4-135); service conductors (NE2-160, NE3-055); GFCI device readily accessible (NE2-012, 042; NE4-012). Row 1740's "lower id" also means catalog file order, so R3-125 comes before NE4-121 (file positions 124 and 1232) | assumptions 1740; code-rules.json order |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Values the Automatic rules read that no needs string names (the row 1742 pattern; until set they behave as Needs data) | Panel: placed position and facing; Panel width and depth (NE2-021, 028); Bus rating (NE3-161); Fed from circuit (NE2-127, 128, NE3-161); Service conductors, size and copper or aluminum (NE2-160, NE3-055). Circuit: Shares a neutral with (NE2-036; it extends NE2-037/NE3-005's needs field). Electric utility point: circuit and protection (row above). Garage room: Vehicle bays (NE2-066, 111). Tub fixture: Hydromassage (NE4-098, 099, 101). Receptacle: Replacement (NE3-138–141); Tamper-resistant (NE3-140, 156); Weather-resistant and In-use cover (NE3-141, 151, 152). Light: Luminaire type and Mounting, which is NE3-175's needs field (also read by NE3-171, 176, 177, 194). Ceiling fan: Blade diameter. Taken from other rules' needs fields: Fan-rated box from NE4-029 (for NE3-082); IC-rated from EN-016 (for NE3-188 to 190); Wiring method from NE3-090 (for NE2-016, NE3-099 and the wiring-method Reminders); countertop listed assembly from NE3-144 (for NE3-146). EZD-620 adds the new ones if it has not merged. Otherwise the first ticket in build order adds them as optional fields, so no version step is needed (EZD-601 precedent): EZD-630 the panel, utility-point and shared-neutral fields; EZD-631 Vehicle bays; EZD-636 the receptacle fields; EZD-637 the luminaire fields and Blade diameter; EZD-639 Hydromassage | assumptions 1742, 1743, EZD-601 rows; catalog check text |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Tamper-resistant and weather-resistant on a New receptacle | Set to yes by default for every New 15 or 20 A receptacle (TR), and for every New outdoor or damp receptacle (WR, plus In-use cover when wet). E-13 says they are "Marked tamper-resistant in the device schedule". A user can set one to no, and then NE3-140/156 or NE3-141/151/152 warn unless an exception in the check text applies (over 66 in., part of a luminaire or appliance, a dedicated appliance space, a non-grounding replacement) | 09b E-13; catalog NE3-140, 151, 152, 156 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Rules on things the model cannot draw that Q-0789 did not add | Registered, never run (or only the named part is not run), and listed in the closing "Not checked by EZ Draft" line, with the AC-1 fixture "reports not checked" (row 645 pattern). Accessory or detached buildings: NE2-046 (accessory part), 112, 122 (accessory part), 136–141, NE3-018. Crawl space: NE2-048, 058. Basement: NE2-049, 113. Boathouse: NE2-053. Sunroom: NE2-005. Foyer: NE2-115. Porch, deck or balcony: NE2-108. Disconnect symbol: NE4-067. EV charger: NE4-087, 090–095. Home battery and ESS: NE4-084, 085, 086. Solar PV: NE4-082, 083. Fixed electric space heater and baseboard: NE4-044, 046, 047, 049. Heated floor or wall cables and mats: NE4-051–056. Room air conditioner: NE4-066, 070–073. Central vacuum: NE4-022. Trash compactor: NE4-026. Sump pump: the sump part of NE2-059 and NE4-011. Microwave and wall oven (not fixture kinds): their parts of NE2-059, NE4-018, 027, 037. Fire alarm panel: NE4-125. Portable generator inlet: NE4-079, 080. Fiber: NE4-131. Attic receptacle for an attic unit (NE2-116, when M-009 is yes): a Checklist item. EV Reminders NE4-008 and NE4-096 appear when EZD-607's loads[] holds a New ev_charger entry. Question 2 below | catalog applies_to; assumptions 645; 09-calculations-and-rules.md#Q-0789 scope ("Rule ids unrelated to these objects stay not checked") |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Ids that phase 57 switches on or widens (Q-0789), registered so phase 57 can switch them on | NE2-124 (633) and NE3-012 (634) wait on the stair (EZD-646). NE2-122's stairway part does too, while its hallway and garage parts run now. NE4-074, 075, 076, 077 and 081 wait on the standby generator (EZD-658); NE4-104 to 114 wait on the pool and spa (EZD-660), as row 1862 says. Each is registered with the phase 57 object kind it waits on, and its AC-1 fixture is "reports not checked". The phase 57 ticket writes its check, even if EZD-644, 655 or 659 merged first, so each evaluator has one owner. These run now and are widened later: NE2-031 (Garage rooms; carports EZD-651), NE2-116 and NE4-020 (air handler and condenser; gas furnaces EZD-656). NE4-078 also waits on the standby generator but is missing from EZD-658's list (note below). NE2-083 (gas fireplace on a 10 A circuit) and NE2-123 (a vehicle door is not an entrance, so EZD-650 garage doors are excluded) run as built | phase-57.md EZD-646, 651, 656, 658, 660; assumptions 1862 |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Reminder triggers for NEC (row 1737 applies) | Box, device, wiring and "every switch/receptacle/luminaire" Reminders: any New or Demo electrical device. Panel Reminders: a New panel, or a New circuit on it. Service Reminders (NE2-144–149, 153, 155, 158, 161; NE3-015, 017, 019–026; NE4-129): a New main panel, a changed service size, or EZD-615's case Service upgrade or Like-for-like panel replacement. Overhead service is not modeled, and the text says so. Wiring-method Reminders (NE3-087–089, 091, 092, 102–110; NE4-132): a circuit whose wiring method is that method. Multiwire Reminders (NE2-038, 039; NE3-049): a circuit with Shares a neutral set. Low voltage (NE4-115–120, 127, 128, 130): a New low-voltage device (EZD-602 kinds; coax means TV). Hydromassage (NE4-097, 100, 102, 103): a New hydromassage tub. Load-calc Reminders (NE2-132, 134, 135; NE3-198; NE4-007, 009, 136, 137): a New load in EZD-607's loads[], a New panel, or a service upgrade. Each plan lists every Reminder id with its trigger | assumptions 1737; S06 Load calc case select |
| EZD-630 to EZD-639 | Wording for Josh to confirm (no board) | Electrical toolbar item "Panel". Panel fields "Panel width", "Panel depth", "Bus rating", "Fed from circuit", "Service conductors", "Copper" / "Aluminum". Electric point "Circuit". Circuit "Shares a neutral with". Receptacle "Replacement", "Tamper-resistant", "Weather-resistant", "In-use cover". Light "Luminaire type" (Enclosed, Open lamp, Pendant, Track, Cord-connected), "Mounting" (Surface, Recessed). Fan "Blade diameter". Tub "Hydromassage (jetted)". Garage "Vehicle bays". Wiring method values "NM cable", "AC cable", "MC cable", "SE cable", "EMT", "PVC conduit", "ENT", "Flexible metal conduit", "Liquidtight metal conduit", "Liquidtight nonmetallic conduit". Notes "{room} is treated as a {class} for NEC rules", "Voltage drop {p}% (advisory; the NEC recommends 3%)", "Neutral required at this switch box". Checklist texts "Grounding electrode conductor: {n} AWG copper for a {s} A service", "Equipment ground: {n} AWG copper for a {b} A breaker", "Listed floor box required", "Show an access panel for the tub pump", "Provide for a future receptacle at the island". Cannot-check "Panel not placed on the plan" | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |
| EZD-630 | Panel position (spec E-16 wants a panel location check; EZD-604's row draws the home run as an arrow because "the panel has no plan position") | The panel is placed on a wall from the Electrical device toolbar, as a "Panel" item in the EZD-602 style, and drawn with the standard panel mark (Q-0705). Its facing is the room side of its host wall. EZD-630 builds it; the panel schema already has a point (EZD-601), and the facing is an optional field, so there is no version step. A panel that is not placed gives cannot-check "Panel not placed on the plan" for NE2-019 to 028, NE2-152, NE3-010 to 012 and NE3-007. It is indoors when it lies inside a room outline. Question 1 lists it with the other unboarded fields | 09b E-16; assumptions EZD-604 home run row; 05-screens.md#Q-0705; 09-calculations-and-rules.md#Q-0789 principle |
| EZD-630 | NE2-020 to 022 and 025, working space | A rectangle in front of the panel's face, as wide as Panel width or 30 in., whichever is greater, 36 in. deep, from the floor to 78 in. It fails when a wall, cabinet box (EZD-402), countertop (EZD-411), fixture, HVAC object, a door's swing in that room, or a soffit or beam lower than 78 in. falls in it, or when the ceiling is under 78 in. An Existing panel of 200 A or less passes the height part (the NE2-022 note's exception). With Panel width not set, 30 in. is used. Fixture: a base cabinet box 35 in. in front of the face warns and 36 in. passes; a 29 in. alcove warns; a 7 ft 6 in. ceiling passes and 6 ft 5 in. warns. NE2-019 (the panel door path) runs as a Checklist item because the panel door is not modeled. One result per panel (grouping row) | catalog NE2-019–022, 025 check and note |
| EZD-630 | NE2-027 light at the panel; NE2-028 dedicated space | NE2-027: a light device in the panel's room with a switch leg from a switch (EZD-605). That is stricter than "6 ft above the panel", because the panel's top is not modeled. A supply or return register, plumbing fixture, water heater or soffit overlapping it fails. It is cannot-check until Panel width and depth are typed | catalog NE2-027 note, NE2-028 note |
| EZD-630 | NE2-036 multiwire source and legs | With Shares a neutral with set, both circuits are on the same panel and on opposite legs. Per EZD-604's numbering, spaces n and n + 2 on one side are the two poles of a 2-pole breaker, so they are opposite legs. Fixture: partners on another panel warn; spaces 5 and 7 pass; spaces 5 and 9 warn | catalog NE2-036; assumptions EZD-604 2-pole numbering |
| EZD-630 | NE2-016 termination rating | The 60 °C pairs from its note: 14 AWG Cu 15 A, 12 AWG 20 A, 10 AWG 30 A, 8 AWG 40 A, 6 AWG 55 A. NM is always 60 °C, and nothing in the model marks equipment as 75 °C, so every circuit uses the 60 °C column. Fixture: 8 AWG Cu on a 50 A breaker warns and 6 AWG passes (also NE3-099 by grouping) | catalog NE2-016 check and note |

| EZD-631 | NE2-050 and NE2-059 by edition | NE2-050 at 8th ed.: only receptacles serving the countertop, or within 6 ft of a sink. At 9th: every Kitchen receptacle. At 8th: the dishwasher only. Fixture: an unprotected dryer warns at 9th and not at 8th, and an unprotected dishwasher warns at both | catalog NE2-050, NE2-059 edition_difference |
| EZD-631 | NE2-063 to 066 and 068 required circuits | NE2-063/099: Kitchen countertop and wall receptacles on at least two 20 A circuits. It may serve several baths' receptacles, or one bath's receptacles, light and fan, and nothing else. NE2-066: a 20 A garage circuit, with outdoor receptacles allowed; the 9th ed. single-bay exception reads Vehicle bays. NE2-068: a 1-pole 15 or 20 A circuit (10 A at 9th) with any device in a Kitchen, Living, Bedroom, Closet or storage, Hallway or Laundry room needs AFCI; one serving only Bath, Garage or outdoor devices does not | catalog NE2-063–068 |
| EZD-631 | NE2-074, 076, 082 loads | NE2-074: a range of 8.75 kW or more (electric point volts × amps, or its catalog rating) on a circuit under 40 A warns. Fixture: 2,500 VA on a 20 A 1-pole circuit warns and 2,400 VA passes. | EZD-632 | NE2-092 to 105 receptacle placement | Wall line: each room edge at the finished face, broken by door openings (every door is a swing door in the model) and by fixed cabinets with no countertop. Receptacles that do not count: over 66 in.; inside a cabinet box's outline at a height within the box; fully switched by the NE2-120 exception; serving a countertop (NE2-096). Countertop top height is the height of the base boxes under it plus its thickness (EZD-411 trace). A receptacle serves a countertop when it lies on the wall line behind it and is no more than 20 in. above the top. Island: a countertop piece with no wall edge. Peninsula: wall edges along one short end only. NE2-101 breaks the run at sink and cooktop cutouts and at range and refrigerator outlines. Fixtures from the check text: 24 in. and 48 in. spacing; 12 in. and 18 in. behind sinks; 20 in. height; 9 and 18 sq ft | catalog NE2-092–105; countertop.ts; 09-calculations-and-rules.md#Q-0559 |

NE2-104: a receptacle 10 in. below an island top with an overhang of 6 in. or less passes at 8th and warns at 9th | catalog NE2-103, NE2-104 edition_difference; 09b E-03 |

NE2-116: a receptacle on the same floor within a straight 300 in. (25 ft) of each New air handler or condenser; 300 in. passes and 300 1/16 in. warns. "Not on the load side of the disconnect" is named in the plan | catalog NE2-111, 114, 116 |

## Phases 48-55 answers (2026-11-20)

| Ticket | Question or text | Choice made | Source |

| EZD-1502, EZD-1503 | Measurement check on the AI Spanish (Q-0792) | After drafting, the job compares the numbers in the English and the Spanish: every run of digits with its fraction or decimal ("34 1/2", "2700", "120", "15") and every unit or code abbreviation token the prompt keeps (", ', V, A, K, GFCI, AFCI, NEC, FBC) must appear in the Spanish the same number of times. If not, text_es stays null, drafted_at is set, and the phrase is marked number_mismatch (new nullable column on translation); crew see the English (EZD-1503 fallback). A later English change redrafts. No notification | 10-permissions-and-settings.md#Q-0792 |
| EZD-1502 | AC added (Q-0792) | A stub draft that changes 34 1/2" to 34" is not stored: the task keeps text_es null and the Spanish user sees the English; a stub draft that keeps every number is stored | 10-permissions-and-settings.md#Q-0792 |
| EZD-1618 | Allowance budgets (Q-0793) | New table allowance_budget (tenant_id, project_id, allowance_category_id, amount numeric(12,2) at least 0, updated_by, updated_at), unique per project and category; RLS and a two-company test; amount is in MONEY_FIELDS as a sell-side value. Typed in Project settings › Allowances (a new section listing the company's allowance categories, EZD-1609) by members with See sell prices; no edit lock (not model data). The selections PDF allowance page (EZD-1609) adds a Budget column and "Over by $x" or "Under by $x" per category with a budget; categories with no budget show items and total only | 05-screens.md#Q-0793; assumptions EZD-1609 allowance summary row; 13b R2 |
| EZD-1618 | Wording for Josh to confirm | Section "Allowances"; column "Budget"; "Over by {amount}"; "Under by {amount}"; "On budget"; "No allowance categories yet. Add them in Settings › Catalog." | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |
| EZD-1711 | Wording for Josh to confirm (Q-0795) | Banner "Your subscription has ended. You can view and export projects for {n} more days."; after 14 days "Your EZ Draft subscription isn't active. Your projects are safe and come back when you pay."; client link "This link isn't available." | CLAUDE.md rule 6 |

| Ticket | Question | Choice | Source |

| EZD-416 | Where the countertop left panel lives | While the Countertops tool is on, the countertop panel (tabs Pieces, Edges, Material; footer totals) takes the plan's left panel; the screen, the panel and the inspector share one countertop selection (piece and edge), so a row selects its top on the canvas and a canvas click marks its row. | EZD-416 | Footer and status bar numbers | Over the tops on the open floor: pieces, seams (a seam shared by two pieces counts once), total area to one decimal "56.1 sq ft", exposed edge to the whole foot "36 lf", half up. Cutouts do not change area (EZD-413 row). Increment is the Defaults countertop increment ("1/2"", 1/2" while not read) | board S04bCountertops; 09f; 09i Countertops; EZD-411, EZD-413 rows |

| EZD-416 | Edges tab rows | One row per edge, grouped under "A · Back wall": "Side n", type Exposed, Wall or Splash (a wall edge with a splash is Splash; a seam or joint is Wall), "· seating" on a seating edge, overhang, length. A row selects the piece and that edge (EZD-414 uses the edge) | S04b Decisions (Edges tab) |
| EZD-416 | Material settings: per top or for the job | One setting for every top on the floor, as the board's single Material section; a value that differs between tops shows "Mixed". Edits need Edit model and the lock, one undo step each | board S04bCountertops; S04b Decisions (Material tab) |
| EZD-416 | Material and backsplash material | The swatch row and the Material tab's Material button show the chosen catalog item's name with the thickness ("Quartz · 3 cm"; "No material chosen · 3 cm" with none; "Catalog material" while the catalog is not read or the item is gone). Either opens the catalog picker on the Countertops category, in a layer beside the rail (the left panel is too narrow for its two columns), titled "Pick a countertop" as EZD-419; a pick sets the item on every top on the floor in one undo step, with Edit model and the lock. Backsplash material reads "Same as top" and is not editable: the model has no separate splash material (board "4" · same") | S04b Controls "Material swatch row ... Opens catalog picker (category counter)"; EZD-401 picker; EZD-419 run inspector; 03c countertop_piece |
| EZD-416 | Thickness, backsplash height, behind range | Thickness picks 2 cm or 3 cm (any other stored value is kept as an option). Backsplash height is typed and set on every wall edge that has a splash (seams and joints keep none). Behind range is a new optional piece field `behindRange` ("splash" = Backsplash height, the reading when absent; "full_height" = Full height); the board's "Full height" is sample data | S04b Controls Backsplash row, Behind range row; 03c |

| EZD-1416 | How the last wall fills itself and how the closure is measured | Each wall runs along one axis of the outline as drawn; the missing wall's axis gives its length, the other axis is the check; the gap is the straight-line distance of the two misses. The filled length is worked out each time, never stored, so it follows later edits; a wall that would come out at zero or less is not filled and the gap shows. A free outline with a wall placed off 90° (held) gets no fill or check | S17b Auto-fill wall, Closure check; Q-0569 c |
| EZD-1416 | Closes, Off by and amber | 1/64" or less shows Closes (03g); any larger gap shows Off by X to 1/16", never 0" (Q-0570); amber only when the gap is above the company Measurement tolerance (1/2" while Not set, as Import review); equal is not amber | S17b Closure check; S15d; Q-0568 |
| EZD-1416 | Keypad behaviour | Keys type into the selected wall's field; the first key on a measured wall starts a new value; a fraction or number after a number or foot mark gets a space ("10' 4 1/2"); Next wall keeps the value and goes to the next wall, or on the last wall to the first wall still without a length (stays when none); tapping another wall keeps a valid typed value; an emptied field clears the wall; text that is not a length stays in the field with the refusal line. The keypad types walls only; the ceiling keeps its own field. Hidden when view only | S17b Keypad, Next wall; board S17bFieldMeasure |
| EZD-1416 | Walls done counter and grey values | The counter counts the filled wall as done; the filled wall's length is grey on the canvas and in the wall field; the wall that will fill shows a grey dash and the badge names it ("E fills in when the room closes", the board's text) | S17b Auto-fill wall; board |
